# Backend Execution Plan — engine reference

## 0. Position and authority

This engine turns the module's **functional truth** (the SRS) and **structural truth** (the
db-script) into one agent-ready backend execution plan. It reads its inputs from
`_state/` only (the generated current state — never a raw `v{N}/` folder), and it [T:brief-state-only]
never invents business meaning, tables, columns, rules or IDs. [C:C7.7]

- Upstream artifacts govern. A conflict between this plan and the SRS or db-script is a
  **finding**, never a silent resolution. [C:C7.10]
- Questions are `forbidden` at this stage. Ambiguity is resolved by the rule in
  `factory.yaml → ambiguity` (see §12): non-breaking → ADR (`ADR`) and
  `continue`; breaking → ADR with status
  `BLOCKED` and `stop`.
- Every block in the plan carries `traces=` to the upstream IDs it implements (§6.0). The
  traceability matrix built by `gov.py analyze` must be CLEAN before the pass gate opens.
- The plan is the **sole backend input** of the implementation agent. The frontend stage and
  the test stage read `api-spec-cat.yaml` (§7.5) — the API document this stage derives from the
  plan — never this plan's prose and never a delivered backend; what the implementation later [C:C9.5]
  publishes is held to that document by `api-verify`.

**Delta run (v2).** Read `_state/` as the baseline; emit only ADDED / MODIFIED / [C:C12.2]
REMOVED blocks plus `change-manifest.md`; continue every ID sequence,
never renumber; keep v1 frozen. Rules: shared/VERSIONING.md. [C:C7.9]

## 1. Inputs

| Input | Read from | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `srs` | `_state/current-srs.md` | authoritative functional truth — REQ/AC/ENT/RULE, screens, permissions, lookup keys |
| `db-script` | `_state/current-db-script.md` | authoritative structural truth — tables, columns (DBF), constraints, XM register |
| `registry-srs` | `_state/current-registry-srs.md` | ID ranges already assigned, shared entities, existing lookups, module prefix |
| `registry-db` | `_state/current-registry-db.md` | ID ranges already assigned, shared entities, existing lookups, module prefix |
| `contract` | `_state/current-contract.md` | authoritative structural truth — tables, columns (DBF), constraints, XM register |
| `contracts?` | `_state/current-contracts?.md` | authoritative structural truth — tables, columns (DBF), constraints, XM register |
| `analysis/domain/` steering + `profile.knowledge.files` | `profiles/commerce/knowledge/raw-idea.md` | primary sources cited when a best-practice choice must be made (§12) |

Business policies are not read directly: client policies are embedded in `RULE-*` inside
the SRS. A RULE sourced from a client policy is never resolved unilaterally — a conflict is a [T:blocked-adr]
breaking ambiguity (§12).

If the db-script is absent the run is **GOVERNANCE REDUCED**: declare it in the plan header,
produce a functional-only plan, mark every DB binding `PENDING`, and record an ADR. Never
downgrade silently.

## 2. Mandatory extraction and binding (§2A)

### 2A.0 The fundamental rule

Before writing any phase content, extract and **bind** every concrete value from the inputs.
A plan containing a placeholder (`[TABLE_NAME]`, `[LOOKUP_KEY]`, "uses a sequence",
"see SRS") is incomplete and fails the alignment self-check (§9).

```
NO-INVENTION RULE
  Every table, column, constraint, index and PK-generation object used anywhere in the plan
  MUST exist in the db-script and be cited by its DBF-* (or the exact object name the [C:C7.10]
  db-script declares). Base fields (audit, flag, PK) come from the db-script — not from
  memory, not from templates. Naming conventions are read from the profile:
    flag suffix   : Fl
    audit fields  : createdBy, createdAt, updatedBy, updatedAt
    PK pattern    : {entity}Pk
    PK generation : `identity` (profile.stack.db.pk_generation)
    target dialect: postgresql16 (profile.stack.db.target_dialect)
```

### 2A.1 Pre-generation extraction table

Emit this table first in the run (it is not part of the plan file; it is the working set
every phase binds from):

```
PRE-GENERATION EXTRACTION — CAT v2
── FROM srs ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
ENTITIES      ENT-CAT-<seq> │ exact name │ kind ∈ master | transactional | lookup | config | security
REQUIREMENTS  REQ-CAT-<seq> │ EARS text  │ its AC-CAT-<seq> list (Given/When/Then)
RULES         RULE-CAT-<seq> │ scope ENT │ trigger │ statement │ message per language (ar, en) │ source
SCREENS       every screen entry the SRS declares │ type │ owning ENT │ composite (Search + Entry = ONE screen)
PERMISSIONS   the SRS permission matrix (roles × screens × actions) — actions VIEW/CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE, gateway VIEW
LOOKUPS       every lookup key the SRS names, exactly as written — rule: all LOV values runtime-loaded from the core lookup module (mdl); no hardcoded enums in APIs or field specs; activity presets and capability flags are core settings, read through FeatureFlags [G]
BUSINESS CODE format per master entity — rule: document numbers come from the core sequence module; never generated in a business module
── FROM db-script ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
TABLES        ENT → exact table name
PK GENERATION exact object the db-script declares per table — strategy `identity`: the identity clause as written for postgresql16
COLUMNS       exact column name │ DBF-CAT-<seq> │ declared type │ null │ default
CONSTRAINTS   exact FK / UNIQUE / CHECK constraint names ; INDEXES exact names
XM            XM-CAT-<seq> │ type │ local column (DBF) │ target module · target ENT │ state │ contract item
              (every one becomes ONE block of `INT-C` — R7; nowhere else)
── FROM registries ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
SHARED ENTITIES consumed (owner module, reached via which XM) — not redeclared [G]
EXISTING LOOKUP KEYS (reuse — do not create a duplicate) [G]
ID RANGES already used for API, QR (continue the sequence)
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Any row that cannot be filled → §2A.3.
```

### 2A.2 Binding rules

| Binding | Rule | Forbidden → Required |
|---|---|---|
| PK generation | every PK reference names the exact object from the db-script — strategy `identity` [G] | "auto-generated" → the exact identity clause as declared |
| Column names | every field reference cites the exact column + `DBF-*`; the implementer maps property → column through the DB Alignment Manifest (§4) | a camelCase invention → `DBF-*` lookup |
| Rule text | every RULE cited in a phase carries its full statement, trigger and message in every language (ar, en) — the plan is self-contained | "applies RULE-… see SRS" → full text inline |
| Lookup keys | the exact key string from the SRS, confirmed against the db-script column that stores it; endpoint per the base path `/api/v1/{module}/{resource}` | a parameter placeholder → the literal key |
| Business code | format stated explicitly (exact pattern from the SRS, column, uniqueness constraint name) | "auto-generated, read-only" → format + column + constraint + generation source |
| Endpoints | every path is an instance of `/api/v1/{module}/{resource}`; verbs mean `POST`=create, `GET`=read, `PUT`=update, `DELETE`=deactivate (soft), `PATCH`=partial | an ad-hoc path → the base-path pattern |

### 2A.3 Extraction failure

A value that cannot be confirmed from the inputs is not invented: [G]

| Case | Action |
|---|---|
| SRS entity has no table in the db-script | mark the entity `PENDING DB` in the plan (GOVERNANCE REDUCED for that entity) + ADR |
| Lookup key / message text / business-code format missing | mark the field `PENDING` with the ADR id; the Error Catalog row carries the ADR id instead of text |
| PK generation object missing for a table | flag in the data phase; QR entry notes `generation: not confirmed`; ADR |
| Two upstream sources contradict | breaking ambiguity → ADR `BLOCKED`, run stops (§12) |

## 3. Plan Index

The plan opens with an index — one table per element family, every row bound from §2A.1:

```
EXECUTION PLAN INDEX — CAT v2 — backend-execution-plan-cat.md
Profile: commerce · dialect: postgresql16 · framework: profile.stack.backend.framework
Open ADRs: <n> — decisions/CAT/

ENTITY REGISTRY   ENT-*  │ name │ table │ business code (if any) │ operations
FIELD REGISTRY    DBF-*  │ property │ read-only? │ ENT-*
API REGISTRY      API-*  │ operation │ verb │ path │ traces (REQ-*, DBF-*)
RULE REGISTRY     RULE-* │ name │ scope │ ENT-* │ message in every language ✓/✗
SCREEN REGISTRY   screen │ type │ ENT-* │ permission names
QRC SUMMARY       QR-*   │ operation │ phase │ ENT-*         (agent reference only — §5) [G]
DB ALIGNMENT      see manifest (§4) — ALIGNED ✓ / issues: <n>
INTEGRATION       <n> edges — one block each in `INT-C`, the last phase (R7)
GIVEN             <n> uses — the SRS A9 `given-usage` rows (erp-core); every one goes through the `via` the row names
SECURITY          <n> screens × <n> roles
```

**Given usage.** The module builds ON `erp-core` (usage skill: `.claude/skills/erp-core-usage@1.6.0` — the executor installs it first; the split
writes the SRS's `given-usage` rows into every package manifest as `given`). The plan's code reaches a given only [C:C-GIVEN-2]
through the `via` a row declares — an interface it calls, an extension point it implements, the API document it
integrates with — and never re-creates an entity or a table the given owns; an operation on a given's entity is a call into [C:C-GIVEN-1]
the given, not a repository of this module. The rows are stated once, in the SRS; this plan cites them and adds none. [C:C-GIVEN-2]

The totals the profile asks this plan to state are ONE block (data, not prose — `gov.py analyze` →
`count-agrees` holds every count to the rows it heads; C0.1 holds the block to its schema): [C:C7.18]

```yaml name=totals
DBF: <the number of distinct `DBF-*` ids in `db-script`>
XM: <the number of distinct `XM-*` ids in `db-script`>
API: <the number of distinct `API-*` ids in `backend-execution-plan`>
QR: <the number of distinct `QR-*` ids in `backend-execution-plan`>
```
Count the rows and state that number once, here. A hand-counted total was the smallest
completeness defect this factory shipped and the one nothing in it counted.

## 4. DB Alignment Manifest

The manifest is the canonical binding between plan fields and db-script fields. It contains
**only** these columns — column names, DB types and SRS references are *sourced by lookup* [C:C7.10]
from the db-script, never reproduced here (duplicating them is a contract violation — [C:C7.10]
shared/ARTIFACT-CONTRACTS.md):

```
DB ALIGNMENT MANIFEST — CAT v2
DBF-*            │ ENT-*          │ plan property │ plan type │ XM-* (if FK crosses modules) │ status
DBF-CAT-001 │ ENT-CAT-001 │ <property>    │ <type>    │ —                            │ ✓
DBF-CAT-007 │ ENT-CAT-001 │ <property>    │ <type>    │ XM-CAT-001               │ ✓
Legend  ✓ aligned · ✗ type mismatch (finding)
A cross-module column is aligned like any other: the id is the whole reference — the
constraint behind it, and what it waits for, are that edge's block in `INT-C` (R7).
Derived / computed properties (no DBF) are listed with DBF = "— (derived)" and an ADR id.
A required column that no endpoint writes carries its reason on the row (system-generated · derived · seeded · DEFERRED · core-provided) — see R3 and `gov.py analyze` → `required-writer`.
```

## 5. Query Reference Catalog (QR-*)

The QRC expresses the **retrieval and persistence intent** of every repository operation as
pseudo-SQL. It is a logical specification, not executable code: the implementer rewrites [G]
every entry with the real entity classes, mapped property names and the project's query
strategy. Copy-pasting a QR entry into production code is a violation.

- Format: `QR-CAT-{seq}` (3-digit sequence, continuous across the module).
- Assigned while writing the data and service phases; every API with a DB operation cites its QR, **and** every QR is cited by ≥1 API — the catalog is checked in both directions (C7.22).
- Ordering / paging use the profile's envelope: `Page<T>`; responses are wrapped in `ApiResponse<T>`.

```
QR-CAT-<seq> — <operation name>
Phase        : <p.key of the phase that defines it>
API          : API-CAT-<seq> — the endpoint that reaches this query (a query reached only through [C:C7.22]
               another QR names the `API-*` at the head of that chain). A QR no `API-*` block cites is a
               query nobody runs: `gov.py analyze` → `orphans` (C7.22)
Entity       : ENT-CAT-<seq>
Operation    : FIND_ONE | FIND_ALL | FIND_BY_CRITERIA | SAVE | UPDATE | DELETE | COUNT | EXISTS | NATIVE | AGGREGATE
Intent       : <what business question this answers / what it must return or change>
Logical spec : SELECT … FROM <exact table> [JOIN <table> ON …] WHERE <conditions from RULE-*> [ORDER BY …] [page/size]
Join         : NONE | required — ADR-<id> (why)
Transaction  : READ_ONLY (reads) | READ_WRITE (writes) | REQUIRES_NEW — ADR-<id> if non-default
Locking      : NONE | <the read that must not be repeatable>: a query whose result is decided on
               and then written back says here what stops a second caller deciding on the same
               result. A read-then-write with no answer on this line is a race, written down
Pagination   : YES (Page<T>) | NO
Filters      : <field: EXACT | LIKE | DATE_RANGE | SET>
Result shape : full entity | projection <fields> | count
Null handling: <per optional field>
```

Two simultaneous requests are the case a specification forgets: the only occurrence of the word [G]
in the whole factory used to be inside the cross-module contract, so the endpoint block and the
catalog entry asked nothing and two races shipped — a unique document number allocated from a
read-then-write, and a guard both parallel requests passed. This is **not** mechanically
checkable and no check pretends to verify it: the question is asked here, and scored at the gate
(`reviewers/pass-review.md`).

Standard operation defaults (apply unless a QR entry overrides them):

| Operation | Default |
|---|---|
| FIND_ONE by PK | read-only; not found → error per `LocalizedException → {code, messageAr, messageEn}` with the catalog row for "not found" |
| FIND_BY_CRITERIA | read-only; filters + allowed sort fields declared per search; empty result → success with empty content, not "not found" [G] |
| SAVE | read-write; PK and audit fields system-set; business code from the numbering rule (document numbers come from the core sequence module; never generated in a business module) |
| UPDATE | read-write; immutable fields (PK, business code, audit) excluded from the request |
| `deactivate (soft)` | usage check first (can-delete / can-deactivate); blocked → catalog error; allowed → `soft` per profile.stack.db.delete_semantics — flip the active flag (suffix `Fl`); the other semantics only where the SRS mandates it [G] |
| EXISTS | read-only uniqueness check; excludes the current PK on update |

Join governance: single-table responses do not join; display names of lookup values are **not** joined — the backend returns the stored code and the frontend resolves the label (all LOV values runtime-loaded from the core lookup module (mdl); no hardcoded enums in APIs or field specs; activity presets and capability flags are core settings, read through FeatureFlags); parent data or cross-entity filters require a join **and** an ADR; cross-entity aggregation may need a native query — say why. [G]

## 6. Phase content

### 6.0 Markers, thresholds, traces — read before writing any phase

Marker grammar (`factory.markers`, schema v2, syntax `html-comment`):
`<!-- KIND:ID:START [traces=…] -->` … `<!-- KIND:ID:END -->`. Kinds that may appear in a
`backend` execution plan:

| Kind | Level | Allowed parents | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PHASE` | 1 | — (top level) [T:marker-foreign-kind] | keys from `profile.tracks.<track>.plans.<plan>.phases` |
| `SUB` | 2 | PHASE [T:marker-foreign-kind] | id = `{PHASE-KEY}-{LABEL}` — always phase-qualified |
| `API` | 3 | PHASE, SUB [T:marker-foreign-kind] | one atom `API-*` = one dedicated block |
| `XM` | 3 | PHASE, SUB [T:marker-foreign-kind] | one atom `XM-*` = one dedicated block |

Rules:
- The first line you write for a phase **is** its `PHASE` START marker; the last line is its
  END marker. Content and markers are one action — not "write, then wrap". [G]
- `traces=`: **every** PHASE, SUB and atom block carries
  `traces=` listing the upstream IDs it implements (comma-separated, grammar
  `{prefix}-{MOD}-{seq}`, 3-digit seq). Obligations from `factory.ids`:
  `API` → REQ + DBF; `XM` → REQ (XM is minted upstream and only placed here). A PHASE block traces to the union of its children. [C:C7.2]
- Split unit is `SUB or PHASE` — never an atom, except in `INT-C`, [T:split-verify]
  which is split per edge: one package per `XM-*` block (R7). Check the
  threshold **while** writing: if the count is already at threshold from §2A.1, open the first
  SUB before its first block. Never write flat and split later.
- Unknown phase key → the toolkit **refuses** (`refuse`). The key [T:phase-unknown]
  is `p.key`, never the display name (the [T:phase-unknown]
  autofix normalises `+ _ space --` to `-` only when unambiguous — do not rely on it). [T:autofix-bounded]
- Any heading containing the word PHASE uses exactly one profile key. Index, manifest, catalog [T:phase-unknown]
  and self-check sections are not phases: distinct headings, no marker, placed before the
  first PHASE or after the last END.
- Full protocol: shared/MARKER-PROTOCOL.md.

Phase table for `profile.tracks.backend.plans.exec` (the plan is organised in exactly this order): [G]

| # | Key | Display | Split rule | Atoms carried |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `CORE` | CORE [T:never-split] | never split | none |
| 2 | `DATA-DOM` | DATA+DOM [T:never-split] | SUB by engine self-check; labels `DATA-DOM-MASTER`, `DATA-DOM-TRANSACTIONAL`, `DATA-DOM-LOOKUP` | none |
| 3 | `SVC-API` | SVC+API [T:never-split] | SUB when API count >= 8 — grouped CRUD / SEARCH / INT; labels `SVC-API-CRUD`, `SVC-API-SEARCH`, `SVC-API-INT` | `API-*` blocks |
| 4 | `DOC` | DOC [T:never-split] | never split | none |
| 5 | `SEC-BE` | SEC-BE [T:never-split] | never split | none |
| 6 | `ALIGN-BE` | ALIGN-BE [T:never-split] | never split | none |
| 7 | `INT-C` | INT-C [T:never-split] | per edge (integration — the last phase) | `XM-*` blocks — one per edge, one package each |


### 6.1 Content roles

The profile names the phases; this engine supplies the content **by role**. Match each
phase to the roles its display name declares (a display such as "SVC+API" declares the
service and API roles). The integration role is not read from a name: it is the phase the
profile flags `integration: true` — `INT-C` — and it is the last phase. A
phase whose display matches no role is filled as the profile describes it. Atom placement is
data-driven: `API-*` blocks go in the phase whose `split_threshold.kind` is `API`; every
`XM-*` block goes in `INT-C` and nowhere else (R7).

**One place for another module.** Outside `INT-C` this plan names another module only by [C:C7.27]
this module's own `XM-*` id (reference-by-id): never the target's table, endpoint, [C:C7.27]
service, operation or id. An endpoint that reads the target says `integrate (XM-CAT-<seq>)`;
what that read is, and how it is wired, is the edge's block (`gov.py analyze` → C7.27).

**R1 — Core / configuration (architecture policies).** Declared once, applies to the module:
- Type mapping postgresql16 → language types, stated once as a table (from `profile.stack.db.syntax_map` rows, PK column type included) — a deviation needs an ADR. The table states column types only; the PK-generation clause is not a type (§2A). [G]
- Runtime error-code format: `{MOD}-{http}[-{SLUG}]` (profile.stack.backend.api.error_code_format) — state this string verbatim and make **every** Error Catalog row (§7) an instance of it; the declared format and the emitted codes come from this one profile value, never from free text. [C:C7.11]
- Lookup values: all LOV values runtime-loaded from the core lookup module (mdl); no hardcoded enums in APIs or field specs; activity presets and capability flags are core settings, read through FeatureFlags.
- Numbering: document numbers come from the core sequence module; never generated in a business module.
- Workflow engine: **forbidden**.
- Search contract: the sort fields each screen allows — read from that screen's SRS filter
  list, not widened here; paging Page<T>; an empty result is success. [G]
- Languages: every named entity carries a name per language (ar, en); a single-language artifact is incomplete.
If nothing module-specific applies, write "Standard configuration — no module-specific abstractions".

**R2 — Data + domain.** One entity block per `ENT-*`, every value bound (§2A):
```
### ENT-CAT-<seq> — <exact name>      kind: <master|transactional|lookup|config|security>
BINDINGS   table <exact> · PK <column, DBF> · PK generation `identity` → <exact identity clause> · db-script version
BUSINESS CODE property · column (DBF) · format <exact> · uniqueness constraint <exact name> · generation source
DEFAULT FIELDS per kind (profile.conventions.entity_defaults): master → nameAr, nameEn, code, isActiveFl, createdBy, createdAt, updatedBy, updatedAt; transactional → docNo, docDate, statusCode, createdBy, createdAt, updatedBy, updatedAt; lookup → code, nameAr, nameEn, sortOrder, isActiveFl; config → key, valueAr, valueEn, isActiveFl
FIELDS     DBF-* │ property │ column (exact) │ type (postgresql16) │ null │ read-only │ constraint │ label per language (ar/en)
DTO MEMBERSHIP  create-request excludes / update-request excludes / response includes (PK, business code, audit, flag stated explicitly)
LOOKUP FIELDS  property │ column (DBF) │ exact lookup key │ endpoint (base path /api/v1/{module}/{resource}) — stores the code, not a numeric FK [G]
DOMAIN RULES   RULE-* full text: trigger · statement · message per language · scope (CREATE|UPDATE|DELETE|ALL) · DB enforcement (constraint name | app-level) · owner layer
STATE MACHINE  (if status-bearing) status column (DBF) · values · initial · transitions (trigger, actor) · terminal · invalid-transition RULE
CROSS-MODULE   XM-* ids touching this entity — the id only; the target and how it is reached are its block (R7) [C:C7.27]
OPERATIONS     the operations this entity exposes, drawn from `VIEW` / `CREATE` / `UPDATE` / `DELETE` — every one
               listed here must be named by an `API-*` block that also names this `ENT-*`. An
               operation stated here and built nowhere is a promise the module never keeps [C:C7.20]
               (`gov.py analyze` → `operation-resolves`); an operation this entity does not
               expose is simply left off the line.
REPOSITORY OPS → QR-* list (FIND_ONE, FIND_BY_CRITERIA, SAVE, UPDATE, EXISTS, …)
```
Grouping for `DATA-DOM`: when the entity count justifies a split (engine self-check — not
marker-countable), group under `SUB:DATA-DOM-MASTER / SUB:DATA-DOM-TRANSACTIONAL / SUB:DATA-DOM-LOOKUP`.

**R3 — Service + API.** One `API-*` block per endpoint, each its own atom marker:
```
<!-- API:API-CAT-<seq>:START traces=REQ-CAT-<seq>,DBF-CAT-<seq> -->
### API-CAT-<seq> — <operation>
Entity       : ENT-CAT-<seq>   (the entity whose OPERATIONS line this endpoint answers) · operation <VIEW|CREATE|UPDATE|DELETE>
Endpoint     : <instance of /api/v1/{module}/{resource}>   verb: <POST|GET|PUT|DELETE|PATCH>
Layers       : <entry layer → method> ; <service layer → method>        (names per R1)
Request      : path params · query params (filter names = properties from R2) · body DTO fields — each names its `DBF-*` (type, required, constraint) · excluded system fields
Response     : status · DTO fields · paginated? (Page<T>) · envelope ApiResponse<T>
Validations  : RULE-* full text (statement, trigger, message per language) — every RULE listed here has a catalog row (§7)
Errors       : catalog rows this endpoint can raise (code, HTTP, RULE-*)
Orchestration : load → validate (RULE-*) → integrate (XM-*) → persist (QR-*, table, generation object)   — WHAT in sequence, layer placement per R1; every column this endpoint writes that the request does not carry (a flag it flips, a status it advances) names its `DBF-*` here
Repository   : QR-* · operation · join (NONE | ADR) · transaction
Concurrency  : NONE — this endpoint neither allocates a unique value nor reads-then-writes
               | <the guard, named>: what two simultaneous requests must not both be allowed to
               do, and what makes that impossible (a unique constraint, a locking read, an
               atomic allocation). Required for every endpoint that allocates a unique value or
               decides on a value it then writes. "Validated first" is not an answer: two
               requests both validate, both pass, both write.
Security     : screen · permission name (`PERM_<PAGE_CODE>_<ACTION>`) — enforced before processing
Localization : every message in ar + en; every name field per language
<!-- API:API-CAT-<seq>:END -->
```
**Honour this module's own contract.** Every API block implementing one of its operation
items states `Honours: CON-CAT-<seq>` (`gov.py analyze` → C7.24 `contract-honoured`).

**Every operation the SRS names needs an endpoint.** Each screen requirement's `Operations`
line is the module's demand, read from the SRS — never from this plan, which cannot both [C:C7.23]
state its own demand and be the proof it was met. Every operation on it is closed here, in
one of three ways and no fourth: an `API-*` that names the operation **and** the `ENT-*` the
screen names on its `Entities` line; or an explicit exclusion carrying one of the profile's stated reasons; or an
ADR that leaves it for a later version — and the gate stays shut. An operation the plan never [C:C7.23]
mentions is the gap this closes: it used to surface at the frontend stage, two stages and one
gate later, when the screen had no endpoint to call. Every `API-*` block therefore carries its
`Entity` line (the `Entities` the screen names) — without it no operation can be resolved to it at all. `gov.py analyze` →
`operation-resolves`.

A `Operations` or `OPERATIONS` line separates the operations it lists with one of
`,` · `;` · `/` · `·` and nothing else. The operation WORDS are this
project's own — the check reads them verbatim, so it never has to anticipate them — but it [C:C7.23]
splits the line on these separators, and one written with any other yields no operation at
all. That failure is silent from the author's side: the clause reports having examined
nothing rather than having failed to read the line.


**Every required column needs a writer.** A column the db-script declares NOT NULL and the
platform does not fill itself must be named by at least one `API-*` block, on its `Request` line
(the caller supplies it) or its `Orchestration` line (the endpoint sets it). A required column no
endpoint writes cannot be satisfied on a fresh deployment, so the first call to every endpoint
that depends on it fails — and no shape check can see it, because the manifest lists the column
and the endpoint lists its fields and nothing joins the two. Where no endpoint should write it,
say so on the manifest row with one of the profile's stated reasons (system-generated · derived · seeded · DEFERRED · core-provided; a seeded one then
belongs in the `bootstrap` block, R5); silence is the defect. `gov.py analyze` → `required-writer`.

Completeness rules: a RULE whose SRS `Data source` is DEFERRED is **not** enforced here — it is
listed once in the entity block as `DEFERRED (no declaration surface in v2)` with no catalog
row, no QR and no enforcing endpoint, because the data its check would read has no column to read
from; every other RULE in Validations ↔ a catalog row (RULE-ERR-CARRY); infrastructure
errors (not found, forbidden, server) are catalog rows with RULE = `PLATFORM-STD` and an ADR; [G]
repository deviations (eager fetch, compound update, native query) need an ADR. Business code
(if any) is excluded from create/update bodies and present in every response. No hard-coded [G]
role checks in services — permission names only. [G]

**R5 — Security (backend half).** Enforced by the profile's security model:
- one block per screen the SRS declares: every API serving it verifies its permission before processing;
- seed data: one row per composite screen in `SEC_PAGES` (page code, name, parent) and one permission row per action `VIEW/CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE` following `PERM_<PAGE_CODE>_<ACTION>`, `VIEW` being the gateway (without it no other permission applies); column names come from the db-script, not from here;
- forbidden responses map through `LocalizedException → {code, messageAr, messageEn}` with a catalog row; [G]
- the permission matrix, ONE block (data, not prose — `gov.py analyze` → `operation-resolves` reads it; C0.1 holds it to its schema), one row per composite screen, the actions the profile's own:

```yaml name=permission-matrix
rows:
  - screen: <screen>
    entity: ENT-CAT-<seq>
    api: [API-CAT-<seq>, …]
    actions:
      VIEW: {granted: true, permission: PERM_<PAGE_CODE>_VIEW}
      CREATE: {granted: true, permission: PERM_<PAGE_CODE>_CREATE}
      UPDATE: {granted: true, permission: PERM_<PAGE_CODE>_UPDATE}
      DELETE: {granted: true, permission: PERM_<PAGE_CODE>_DELETE}
```
  An action is `granted: true` only when the row also names the `API-*` that serves it **and** the [C:C7.20]
  permission name for *that* action. A granted action with nothing behind it grants nothing and is enforced
  by nothing, while reading downstream as a decision that was implemented — every endpoint of
  the shipped module answered forbidden to every caller behind exactly such a matrix [C:C7.20]
  (`gov.py analyze` → `operation-resolves`). Write `granted: false` rather than grant hopefully.


**BOOTSTRAP DATA — the `bootstrap` block.** Structure and behaviour are not enough: a module is unusable until the rows
its first call reads already exist. State them here, one row each, every row naming **who produces
it** (`source`) — not only that it is needed (`gov.py analyze` → `bootstrap-complete`; C0.1 holds the block to its schema): [C:C7.21]

```yaml name=bootstrap
rows:
  - {kind: lookup key, name: "<the lookup key, exactly as `srs` states it>", source: "<who produces it, and where>"} [C:C7.21]
  - {kind: permission, name: "<the permission, exactly as `backend-execution-plan` states it>", source: "<who produces it, and where>"} [C:C7.21]
```
- A lookup key whose table belongs to **another module** is still this module's
  to seed. The seeding block of the structural stage only covers tables this module's own script [C:C7.21]
  creates, so a row left to that block is a row nothing anywhere in the pipeline ever creates —
  the delivered module failed its very first create call against an empty table.
- Registering a page and minting a permission name is **not** a grant. Every permission this
  module declares carries its `role` here (profile.conventions.security_model.grant_target)
  and the seed rows that make the grant real; without it every endpoint answers through
  `LocalizedException → {code, messageAr, messageEn}` to every caller, the administrator included.
- A required column that no endpoint writes (R3) is seeded here or it is a `required-writer` finding.

The frontend stage references these permission names — it does not redeclare them. [G]

**R6 — Alignment (self-check).** The ALIGN table of §9, written as the phase content of the
alignment-role phase (never split). If the profile declares no alignment-role phase, the [T:never-split]
table is trailing content placed before `INT-C` opens — the integration phase is always the last. [C:C7.25]

**R7 — Integration (the last phase, `INT-C`).** The ONE place this plan speaks about another
module. One block per `XM` edge — every edge the db-script register declares (C7.5) and every
one this stage mints — and each block is the only thing an executor reads to wire that edge: [C:C7.26]
no "see the db-script", no row elsewhere to reconcile it with. The lines, in this order, all
mandatory (`factory.yaml → integration`): [C:C7.26]

<!-- RENDER:integration -->
| Line (in this order) | Carries |
|---|---|
| `target` | `{target_module} · {target_entity}` — the owner's module code and `ENT` id (xm.record) |
| `type` | `HARD-FK` · `SOFT-READ` |
| `contract` | `contract-{mod}.md#{item}` — the target's published contract item |
| `requires` | data, not prose: `{target}:DELIVERED` |
| `do` | complete, runnable content — `ddl_patch` · `adapter` · `config` (`ddl_patch` required for `HARD-FK`) |
| `tests` | `TC-*` / `AC-*` ids that verify the edge (non-empty) |
| `if_not_met` | `skip-block; record in execution-state.json → deferred_xm; continue` |

- **Where**: the phase the profile flags `integration: true` in the `exec` plan of every track carrying the `XM` atom — exactly one, the **last** phase; one block per edge. [C:C7.25]
- **Database stage**: column-only — the column, no constraint to a foreign table, and one comment `constraint → XM-…`; the constraint text lives only in the block. [C:C6.15] [C:C7.28]
- **Every other phase**: reference-by-id — this module's `XM-*` id, never the target's tables, endpoints, services or ids. [C:C7.27]
- **Split**: one package per block at `<delivery partition>/integration/XM-…` with `package.json` (`xm`, `target`, `requires`, `tests`, `acceptance`, `given`), listed under `manifest.json → status.split.integration` — never counted toward the module's delivery; the factory records no state for it. [T:graph-derived-state]
<!-- /RENDER:integration -->

```
<!-- XM:XM-CAT-<seq>:START traces=REQ-CAT-<seq> -->
### XM-CAT-<seq> — <dependency>
target     : TARGET · ENT-TARGET-<seq>
type       : HARD-FK | SOFT-READ
contract   : contract-target.md#CON-TARGET-<seq>
requires   : TARGET:DELIVERED
do         :
  ddl_patch : [C:C7.26] ALTER TABLE <this module's table> ADD CONSTRAINT FK_<LOCAL>_<REF> FOREIGN KEY (<column — DBF-CAT-<seq>>) REFERENCES <target table> (<target key>);   (HARD-FK)
  adapter   : [C:C7.26] <the complete adapter — written out, never a pointer elsewhere>
  config    : [C:C7.26] <the complete config — written out, never a pointer elsewhere>
tests      : AC-CAT-<seq>, TC-CAT-<seq>
if_not_met : skip-block; record in execution-state.json → deferred_xm; continue
<!-- XM:XM-CAT-<seq>:END -->
```

- **target / contract.** Plan against what the target PROMISES — its published contract (the
  `contracts` input) — never against a guess at its implementation: the owner's module code and [C:C7.12]
  its `ENT` id, and the contract item the edge depends on (`CON-*`, or the `ENT-*` the contract
  exposes). A target that has published no contract yet is recorded in the register with its
  workaround (shared/XM-PROTOCOL.md §4) and the line anchors on the owner's `ENT-*`;
  `gov.py analyze` → `xref-resolve` decides whether it resolves.
- **requires** is data, never a sentence. The factory does not wait for it and does not check [C:C7.26]
  it at run time: the executor reads it, and when it does not hold follows `if_not_met`.
- **do** is complete. The constraint text is written here in full, generated from this module's
  column (`DBF-*`) and the target's contract — the db-script carries only the column and its [C:C6.15]
  pointer `constraint → XM-CAT-<seq>` (column-only); the text exists nowhere
  else (`gov.py analyze` → `patch-single-source`). The adapter is
  the target module's published in-process interface, injected (`profile.conventions.module_interface: in_process`) —
  name the interface and the operation on it. This platform is ONE deployable: there is no
  HTTP client, no base path, no timeout and no network error path, so no Error Catalog row
  (§7) may describe a network failure that cannot occur.
  A structural (foreign-key) row's access is the key itself and follows from its
  classification, not from this line. Whatever the mechanism, the thing named must be one
  the target module really publishes — `gov.py analyze` → `xref-resolve` resolves every
  foreign id against that module's own registry. If it does not exist yet, the register records the edge with its workaround — never a prose [C:C7.12]
  promise such as "through the target's read APIs".
- **tests** name what proves the edge: the `AC-*` of the requirements it traces — the criteria
  the test stage derives the edge's `TC-*` from — and those `TC-*` once they exist. Never empty.
- **Minting.** Where this stage's own content — a security role, a rule turned into a runtime
  check — is the first thing that reads another module's data, **mint** the `XM-*` here as
  one more block, continuing the atom's sequence: the register was frozen before that dependency
  existed, and a dependency that may not be written down is one that nothing tracks. The
  orchestrator back-registers the minted edge into `_state`'s register in the same
  run, with the state the dependency graph derives for it (shared/XM-PROTOCOL.md §3); a minted
  edge left out of the register is a finding (`gov.py analyze` → C7.5b), minting itself is not.
- **Inbound** dependencies are not written here: every module's edges toward CAT are in the
  dependency graph (`gov.py graph` → `platform/dependency-graph.json`), and what CAT promises them
  is its published contract (P1.5). The dependency's own state lives in the register
  (shared/XM-PROTOCOL.md §4); this phase carries no state at all.

### 6.2 Phase-by-phase instructions

#### PHASE 1 — `CORE` (CORE)
- Open with `<!-- PHASE:CORE:START traces=… -->`, close with `<!-- PHASE:CORE:END -->`.
- Content: the roles in §6.1 whose words appear in "CORE"; otherwise as the profile describes this phase.
- Split: [T:split-verify] never — level-1 only, no SUB.
- Atoms: none — entity/rule blocks carry no marker of their own.

#### PHASE 2 — `DATA-DOM` (DATA+DOM)
- Open with `<!-- PHASE:DATA-DOM:START traces=… -->`, close with `<!-- PHASE:DATA-DOM:END -->`.
- Content: the roles in §6.1 whose words appear in "DATA+DOM"; otherwise as the profile describes this phase.
- Split: [T:split-verify] by engine self-check, labels `DATA-DOM-MASTER`, `DATA-DOM-TRANSACTIONAL`, `DATA-DOM-LOOKUP`.
- Atoms: none — entity/rule blocks carry no marker of their own.

#### PHASE 3 — `SVC-API` (SVC+API)
- Open with `<!-- PHASE:SVC-API:START traces=… -->`, close with `<!-- PHASE:SVC-API:END -->`.
- Content: the roles in §6.1 whose words appear in "SVC+API"; otherwise as the profile describes this phase.
- Split: [T:split-verify] open `<!-- SUB:SVC-API-<LABEL>:START traces=… -->` groups when the `API` count is >= 8, grouped CRUD / SEARCH / INT; labels `SVC-API-CRUD`, `SVC-API-SEARCH`, `SVC-API-INT`. Every atom then sits inside a SUB — no orphan atoms beside SUBs.
- Atoms: one `API-*` marker pair per atom, `traces=` on each.

#### PHASE 4 — `DOC` (DOC)
- Open with `<!-- PHASE:DOC:START traces=… -->`, close with `<!-- PHASE:DOC:END -->`.
- Content: the roles in §6.1 whose words appear in "DOC"; otherwise as the profile describes this phase.
- Split: [T:split-verify] never — level-1 only, no SUB.
- Atoms: none — entity/rule blocks carry no marker of their own.

#### PHASE 5 — `SEC-BE` (SEC-BE)
- Open with `<!-- PHASE:SEC-BE:START traces=… -->`, close with `<!-- PHASE:SEC-BE:END -->`.
- Content: the roles in §6.1 whose words appear in "SEC-BE"; otherwise as the profile describes this phase.
- Split: [T:split-verify] never — level-1 only, no SUB.
- Atoms: none — entity/rule blocks carry no marker of their own.

#### PHASE 6 — `ALIGN-BE` (ALIGN-BE)
- Open with `<!-- PHASE:ALIGN-BE:START traces=… -->`, close with `<!-- PHASE:ALIGN-BE:END -->`.
- Content: the roles in §6.1 whose words appear in "ALIGN-BE"; otherwise as the profile describes this phase.
- Split: [T:split-verify] never — level-1 only, no SUB.
- Atoms: none — entity/rule blocks carry no marker of their own.

#### PHASE 7 — `INT-C` (INT-C)
- Open with `<!-- PHASE:INT-C:START traces=… -->`, close with `<!-- PHASE:INT-C:END -->`.
- Content: the roles in §6.1 whose words appear in "INT-C"; otherwise as the profile describes this phase.
- Split: [T:split-verify] per edge — no SUB; the splitter emits one package per `XM-*` block (R7). This is the last phase.
- Atoms: one `XM-*` marker pair per edge, `traces=` on each, every line of R7 in order.

## 7. Error Catalog

Canonical, produced with the service/API role, kept in **one** location (a pointer elsewhere
is fine; a second table is a duplicate). Envelope: `LocalizedException → {code, messageAr, messageEn}`.

ONE block (data, not prose — `gov.py analyze` → `code-format`, `api-spec-errors` and the API document's derivation read it; C0.1 holds it to its schema):
```yaml name=error-catalog
rows:
  - {code: "<runtime value per envelope>", rule: RULE-CAT-<seq> | PLATFORM-STD, api: [API-CAT-<seq>], http: <status>, trigger: "<when it fires>", messages: {ar: "<message>", en: "<message>"}, adr: "<ADR id when rule is PLATFORM-STD>"}
```
- The `http` value is **not free text**: it holds one of
  `200`, `201`, `400`, `401`, `403`, `404`, `409`, `413`, `415`, `422`, `500` (profile.stack.backend.api.http_statuses) — the statuses this
  platform can actually emit. A row on any other status is raisable by no code path in the
  platform, however well-formed its code string is; `gov.py analyze` → `code-format` refuses it. [C:C7.11]
  If a row genuinely needs a status outside the set, that is a platform change and an ADR, never a [C:C7.11]
  catalog row written anyway.
- Every RULE that produces a user-facing message has a row; message text is copied
  character-perfect from the SRS in every language (ar, en); a missing
  language → `PENDING ADR-<id>`, not invented. [G]
- Downstream consumers (frontend plan, test plan, api-verify) cite the **code**; they do not [G]
  reproduce message text.
- The runtime code format (as the framework serialises it) is stated once in R1 as
  `{MOD}-{http}[-{SLUG}]` so that api-verify can assert on it. The declaration and the rows are the
  same fact: a code that is not an instance of the declared format, or a declared format
  no row obeys, is a finding (`gov.py analyze` → `code-format`), never a convention to [C:C7.11]
  inherit into the next module.

## 7.5 API document — `api-spec-cat.yaml`

Emitted in the same run, **after** the plan and the catalog, as `api-spec-cat.yaml`: an
**OPENAPI 3.1.0** document DERIVED from R3 and §7, never written beside [C:C7.29]
them. `gov.py analyze` proves the two agree (C7.29 `api-spec-agree`, C7.30 `api-spec-errors`,
C7.31 `api-spec-valid`); the frontend stage binds its plan to it and the test stage cites it, so
neither waits for a built backend; the frontend executor serves it from a mock server
(`mock: api-spec-cat.yaml` in the frontend plan's `api-surface` block — the factory emits the
document and the line, it runs nothing); `api-verify` holds the delivered api-docs to it.

The derivation, line by line — every value is READ from the plan or the profile:

| Source | Becomes |
|---|---|
| every `API-*` block (R3) | exactly one operation under `paths`: the `Endpoint` line gives the path (as written — an instance of `/api/v1/{module}/{resource}`) and the method (its `verb:`); the block's id on `x-api-id`, its `traces=` on `x-traces`, its heading as `operationId` / `summary` [C:C7.29] |
| the block's `Request` line | `parameters` (path · query, `required` as the line says) and, for a body-bearing method, `requestBody` — every field typed from its `DBF-*` through the db-script's type (`syntax_map`) |
| the block's `Response` line | the success response under its status with the DTO fields as a schema, envelope `ApiResponse<T>`; a paginated response carries `x-paginated: true` and the query parameters `page`, `size` (`profile.stack.backend.api.paging_params`) |
| every §7 row that names the block (its `API-*` column / the block's `Errors` line) | one response keyed by the row's `HTTP`, the row's code listed under `x-error-codes` — one row, ≥1 operation; a row naming no block is answered by every operation that can raise it |
| the profile's security scheme | `components.securitySchemes` with `http · bearer · JWT` and one document-level `security` requirement every operation is under (`profile.stack.backend.api.auth`) |

Coverage the document must reach (`factory.api_spec.required`): `paths` · `operations` · `request_schemas` · `response_schemas` · `error_responses` · `paging` · `auth`.
A value the plan does not state is a **plan defect**: fix the block or the catalog, never guess in [C:C7.29]
the document. An operation with no block, a block with no operation, a method or path the two state
differently, a catalog row no response answers, a document the standard rejects — each is a C7.29 /
C7.30 / C7.31 finding, and CRITICAL keeps the gate closed. A delta version re-emits the document
**whole** (it is generated — `fold: replace` in `factory.yaml`), never as a delta of records. [T:fold-carry]

## 8. Security

Covered by R5 (§6.1) — permission names follow
`PERM_<PAGE_CODE>_<ACTION>`, minted only from the SRS permission matrix; [G]
a permission name that appears in the plan but not in the matrix is a finding.
Review check: `profile.review.extra_checks` rows whose stage is `P3.1`:
- `CM-4` (MAJOR): every mutation endpoint declares its PERM_* requirement
- `CM-5` (CRITICAL): every cross-module row states the access mechanism conventions.module_interface declares — no per-row choice, no HTTP client and no network error path where the platform is one deployable
- `CM-10` (CRITICAL): a cross-module dependency points only DOWNWARD in the brief's §5 DAG (a lower-numbered module never depends on a higher one)

## 9. Alignment self-check (ALIGN)

Validates the plan **against itself and its bindings**. Runs automatically after the last
content phase; a ✗ is fixed in the plan before the run ends (the fix is an ADR if it was a
choice).

ALIGN is the *prose* half of the check and it is not the authority. The mechanical half is
`gov.py analyze`, which resolves — across artifacts, and across modules — exactly the things [G]
a prose pass reads past:

| Mechanical check | What it resolves |
|---|---|
| `traces` | every `PHASE`/`SUB`/atom block carries `traces=`, and every `API-*` traces to its `REQ-*` and its `DBF-*` |
| `orphans` (REQ) | every `REQ-*` is covered by at least one `API-*` or `DBF-*` |
| `registry-agree` | every `XM-*` the register declares is placed here, and every `XM-*` minted here is back-registered into it |
| `intc-last` | `INT-C` is the last PHASE, and every `XM-*` block sits inside it |
| `xm-block-complete` | every `XM-*` block carries every line of R7, in order: `requires` in its declared shape, the constraint text for a type that needs it, `tests` non-empty |
| `no-foreign-scatter` | outside `INT-C` no line names another module's table, operation, endpoint or id — only this module's `XM-*` ids [C:C7.27] |
| `patch-single-source` | the constraint text of an edge exists only in its block — not in the db-script, not in another phase [C:C7.28] |
| `value-agreement` | the physical column a `DBF-*` names in this plan is character-identical to the one the db-script declares for it (registry row, `CREATE TABLE`, `COMMENT ON`) |
| `code-format` | every Error Catalog code is an instance of the format R1 declares (`{MOD}-{http}[-{SLUG}]`), and its HTTP status is one the platform can emit |
| `api-spec-agree` | every `API-*` block is exactly one operation of `api-spec-cat.yaml` and every operation one block, agreeing on method and path [C:C7.29] |
| `api-spec-errors` | every Error Catalog row is answered by an operation of `api-spec-cat.yaml` with the row's status and code |
| `api-spec-valid` | `api-spec-cat.yaml` validates against the OPENAPI 3.1.0 schema and reaches every item of `factory.api_spec.required` |
| `data-source` | every `RULE-*` this plan turns into a runtime check has a declared source for the data the check *reads* — or an explicit deferral |
| `xref-resolve` | every id of another module cited here is defined in that module's own registry |
| `operation-resolves` | every operation an SRS screen names is built by an `API-*`, or the plan states why it is not |
| `refs-exist` | every `ADR-*` file this plan cites by path exists on disk in `analysis/decisions/CAT/` |
| `paths-resolve` | every path the generated manifest and execution state emit resolves to something that exists |
| `orphans` (QR) | every catalogued query is reached by at least one `API-*` — the direction the QRC's own four assertions never ran, so a query nobody runs was invisible to all of them [C:C7.22] |
| `count-agrees` | every total this plan states equals the rows it heads, and two statements of the same total agree with each other |
| `required-writer` | every column the db-script requires is written by at least one endpoint's request or orchestration, or carries a stated reason why not |
| `operation-resolves` | every operation an entity declares is answered by an `API-*`, and every marked permission-matrix cell names an `API-*` and the permission for that action |
| `bootstrap-complete` | every lookup key and every permission this plan declares has a row in the `bootstrap` block naming who produces it |

| `verdict-agrees` | the `self-check` block does not claim fewer findings than `analyze` produced for this plan |

**Every row of ALIGN names the check that backs it, and there are no other rows.** The
block used to assert traceability, binding, manifest integrity and coverage in prose that no
check could falsify; four of those rows were false in a delivered module and the verdict beneath
them read clean. A self-check row nothing can falsify is worse than no row — it
manufactures confidence — so a row whose assertion no named check examines was **deleted**, not
softened. Do not add one back: if a dimension matters and no check covers it, the fix is a clause
in `shared/ARTIFACT-CONTRACTS.md`, not a sentence here.

Each row's mark is therefore **the analyze report's result for that check**, copied, not an
independent judgement: you do not adjudicate a row the machine already decided. Where the report
says a clause examined nothing, that row is written `— examined nothing`, never ✓: no finding over [C:C7.15]
an empty set is not a pass, and the COVERAGE row carries the report's own list.

**Do not author the verdict.** The verdict is the `self-check` block below the table — `findings`
and `clean` — and the orchestrator writes it from the analyze report after the stage completes
(`gov.py` → `_stamp_verdict`); `verdict-agrees` refuses any hand-written verdict that [C:C7.15]
claims fewer findings than the machine produced. A self-check that always prints [C:C7.15]
clean transfers false confidence downstream and is worse than no self-check —
so the count is no longer a thing a model is asked to be honest about. Write the block with
`findings: 0, clean: true` as a placeholder; it is overwritten.

```
ALIGN — CAT v2
row               backing check        assertion
TRACEABILITY      traces               every PHASE/SUB/atom block carries traces=, and every API traces to its REQ and its DBF
COVERED           orphans              every REQ is covered by ≥1 API or DBF
BINDING (§2A)     value-agreement      every DBF names the same physical column here as the db-script declares for it
MANIFEST (§4)     count-agrees         every total this plan states equals the rows it heads
WRITERS           required-writer      every required column is written by an endpoint, or the row states why not
QRC (§5)          orphans              every catalogued query is reached by ≥1 API
API (R3)          code-format          every catalog code is an instance of the declared format and carries a status the platform can emit
API DOCUMENT      api-spec-agree       every API block is one operation of api-spec-cat.yaml and every operation one block, agreeing on method and path
ERROR RESPONSES   api-spec-errors      every catalog row is answered by an operation of api-spec-cat.yaml with its status and code
DOCUMENT VALID    api-spec-valid       api-spec-cat.yaml validates against OPENAPI 3.1.0 and reaches every required item
RULE INPUTS       data-source          every RULE enforced at runtime names where the data it READS comes from, or is deferred
CROSS-MODULE      registry-agree       every registered XM is placed here, and every XM minted here is back-registered
INTEGRATION       xm-block-complete    every edge is one complete block of the last phase, and nothing else names its target
FOREIGN IDS       xref-resolve         every id of another module cited here is defined in that module's own registry
BOOTSTRAP         bootstrap-complete   every lookup key and every permission has a row naming who produces it
SECURITY (R5)     operation-resolves   every declared entity operation resolves to an API, and every marked matrix cell names its API and its permission
DEMAND (SRS)      operation-resolves   every operation an SRS screen names is built by an API, or the plan states why it is not
DECISIONS         refs-exist           every ADR this plan cites exists on disk in analysis/decisions/CAT/
PATHS             paths-resolve        every path the generated manifest and execution state emit resolves to something that exists
COVERAGE          (the report)         the clauses the analyze report lists as having examined nothing — verbatim, or `none`
```
```yaml name=self-check
findings: 0          # written by the orchestrator from the analyze report — leave it alone
clean: true
```
Coverage tables (ENT/DBF → phases → QR → XM; RULE → API → catalog code; XM → requires →
tests) close the section.

## 10. Registry update — `registry-exec-be-cat.md`

Written in the same run, after ALIGN (categories: shared/REGISTRY-SCHEMA.md):

```
REGISTRY — P3.1 — CAT v2
ID RANGES        API-CAT-<first>..<last> · QR-CAT-<first>..<last>
ENTITIES / TABLES bound   · lookups reused / new (keys)
INTEGRATION      XM ids, one block each in INT-C · requires per edge
CATALOG          code count · rules without message → ADR ids
API DOCUMENT     api-spec-cat.yaml · operations <n> = API blocks <n> · error responses <n> = catalog rows <n>
ALIGN            verdict as stamped · findings fixed
ADRs             decisions/CAT/ADR-CAT-<seq> … (status)
TRACEABILITY     REQ covered by ≥1 API/DBF: <n>/<total> · orphan REQ: <list — a gate blocker>
```

## 11. Structural self-check (toolkit)

Before the run ends:
```
[ ] every profile key in §6.0 has exactly one PHASE START/END pair, in profile order [T:marker-duplicate]
[ ] every API-*/XM-* mentioned anywhere has exactly ONE dedicated marker pair [T:marker-duplicate]
[ ] `INT-C` is the LAST phase; every XM block sits in it and carries every R7 line in order
[ ] no other phase names a foreign table, endpoint, service or id — only this module's XM-* ids [C:C7.27]
[ ] every SUB id is {PHASE-KEY}-{LABEL}; identical labels under different phases stay distinct
[ ] every PHASE/SUB/atom carries traces=
[ ] no heading label repeats; trailing content (§9–§10 when not a phase) sits after the last PHASE END
[ ] thresholds were checked while writing, not retrofitted
```
Then run the toolkit validation — a non-zero exit is blocking:
```
gov.py split --track backend --module CAT --version 2 --dry-run
```
`gov.py analyze` (traceability matrix, EARS, marker validity, registry ↔ artifact agreement)
runs before the gate `P3.2`; CRITICAL findings keep the gate closed.

## 12. Ambiguity rule (no questions here)

`factory.yaml → ambiguity`, stated once in shared/GOVERNANCE-CORE.md:
- **non-breaking** (does not contradict a locked decision or a REQ) → choose the best-practice
  answer using `profile.knowledge.files` + `analysis/domain/` steering, write
  `analysis/decisions/CAT/ADR-CAT-{seq:03d}.md` (Context / Decision /
  Consequences / traces) and **continue**;
- **breaking** (contradicts a locked decision or a REQ) → ADR with status
  `BLOCKED`, then **stop**; the
  orchestrator surfaces it at the next human point.
Every "STOP and ask" of earlier engine generations is replaced by this rule.

## 13. Boundaries and hand-off

| Owns (mints) | References (read-only) | Never touches |
|---|---|---|
| `API-*`, `QR-*`; DB Alignment Manifest; Error Catalog; QRC; ALIGN rows; ADRs it raises | `POL-*` (P0), `US-*` (P0.5), `REQ-*` (P1), `AC-*` (P1), `ENT-*` (P1), `RULE-*` (P1), `DBF-*` (P2), `XM-*` (P2), `CON-*` (P1.5), `SCR-REQ-*` (P1), `FEAT-*` (feature) | frontend/UX atoms (`UXD`, `SCR` — P3.2), `TC-*` (P4), any code, framework annotations, executable queries, test artifacts |

Hand-off (the orchestrator prints it): the plan + registry are split by the toolkit into
`/backend-execution/` inside the
shared repo after the `P3.2` verdict — nothing is copied anywhere; the implementer reads
it where it was written, at the commit its own repo pins. The implementer reads
the plan in order (index → manifest → ADRs → phases in profile order → QRC → catalog), rewrites
every QR, implements security per R5, and publishes its api-docs, which `api-verify` holds to
`api-spec-cat.yaml` (C11.4) — the frontend did not wait for them. Each `INT-C` block becomes its own package [G]
(`integration/XM-CAT-<seq>` in the delivery partition, `package.json` beside it);
the implementer runs it when its `requires` holds and otherwise follows its `if_not_met`. The
module is delivered by its other packages — the factory neither waits for nor tracks these.

# PART A — MODULE FOUNDATION

## A1 — Document information

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Module | CAT — Catalog / الكتالوج |
| Feature code | CAT |
| Version | v2 (baseline v1; change set CS-CAT-001 ADDITIVE). No SRS exists in the current state, so this SRS is written in full and every P1 sequence starts at 001 |
| Date | 2026-10-26 |

| Prepared by | claude:opus (analysis lane) |
| Decisions applied | 6 ADR (ADR-CAT-038 … ADR-CAT-043) · 15 DEFAULT (D-01 … D-15) |

## A2 — Functional context

### In scope
The catalogue that every channel uses, i.e. what is sold: Items, Variants, Variant attributes and their values, Categories, Bundles (component lines), Item units, Barcodes (GTIN, internal, scale codes), Item media, publish state, tax class and e-invoice data on Items and Variants, the e-invoice unit of each unit of measure, scale label layouts, Excel import of Items, barcode label printing, the catalogue search used by the global search, and catalogue-change events.

### Out of scope
- Prices, promotions and price labels (PRC). CAT holds no price [DP §7.2].
- Stock quantities, lots and dual-quantity movements (INV). CAT holds no stock quantity [DP §7.2].
- Serial numbers and warranty (SWR).
- Owning the capability flags, the activity presets and the setup wizard (owner OPEN, P-02 — ADR-CAT-041).
- Maintaining lookup values: brands and units of measure are kept in the core lookup module (`erp-core:modules.mdl`). CAT uses them and does not rebuild them [DP §5 G6].
- Importing Variants, Bundles or media from Excel. The v2 import takes one Item, with its default Variant, per row.
- Pre-assembled kits, activity-specific catalogue models and multi-vendor catalogues (business-policies SCOPE EXCEPTIONS, per the PRD DEFERRED note).

### Module function
CAT is the single catalogue of a tenant. The owner defines an Item once, and the back-office, the point of sale and the storefront all read that same record. CAT is first in the fixed module order and depends on no business module; downstream modules read it and react to its catalogue-change events [KB:raw-idea.md §5]. What the catalogue does depends only on the capability flags `variants` and `bundles` [KB:raw-idea.md §3 D2, §4.1].

### Detailed description
- **Owner** creates Items with only the Arabic name to start; the other mandatory entries are pre-filled. Under "Advanced" the owner adds the English name, Category, brand, descriptions and e-invoice data. When the `variants` flag is on, the owner describes an Item by up to three Variant attributes, and the system creates one Variant per combination. When the `bundles` flag is on, the owner builds Bundles from existing Variants. The owner also adds units (for example a carton of 12 pieces) and barcodes, uploads images, publishes Items to the storefront, imports an existing item list from Excel at the setup wizard's item-import step, and maintains the catalogue settings (scale label layouts and the e-invoice unit of each unit).
- **Seller/cashier** finds Items through the global search or by scanning a barcode or a scale label, and reads the same Item the back-office maintains.
- **Warehouse staff** read Items, look Variants up by barcode, and print barcode labels.
- **Storefront shopper** (customer role) sees only active, published Items through the storefront module, which reads the catalogue.

### Current situation
| Step | Party | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Item kept in a spreadsheet or a separate POS list | Owner | Same item typed again in each tool [KB:raw-idea.md §1] |
| Online offer kept in a separate e-commerce back-end | Owner | Changes reach each channel by hand |
| Barcodes and weighed goods keyed at the counter | Seller/cashier | No shared barcode list |

### Current difficulties
The same item has to be maintained in three places. Names and codes drift between channels. Setup is slow, because tools demand many mandatory fields. Tools also force functions (variants, kits) on businesses that do not use them [KB:raw-idea.md §2, §6].

### Proposed system and benefits
- One catalogue record per Item, read by every channel (US-CAT-001, US-CAT-002).
- Behaviour driven by flags, with only the enabled functions shown (US-CAT-003, US-CAT-004).
- At most four mandatory entries per screen, with the Item's code assigned automatically (US-CAT-007, US-CAT-009) [KB:raw-idea.md §6.1].
- Excel import inside the setup wizard (US-CAT-010) [KB:raw-idea.md §6.9].

### General notes
- Tenancy is handled by the given core; CAT is tenant-agnostic [DP §5 G4].
- Every error message offers a next action [KB:raw-idea.md §6.5].
- Every identifier is a BIGINT identity the database assigns.
- Lookup values are loaded at runtime from `mdl`, and enumerations are never hard-coded.
- Document and business numbers come from the core sequence module.
- Change history is provided by the given `audit` and has no CAT requirement (PRD P-5).

## A3 — Entities and fields

**Standard fields per kind (profile `conventions.entity_defaults`), listed once and carried by every entity of the kind:**
- master: nameAr, nameEn, code, isActiveFl, createdBy, createdAt, updatedBy, updatedAt
- lookup: code, nameAr, nameEn, sortOrder, isActiveFl
- config: key, valueAr, valueEn, isActiveFl
- Audit fields (createdBy, createdAt, updatedBy, updatedAt) are filled by the system and never accepted from a caller.
- Dependent records (ENT-CAT-005, 007, 008, 009, 010) take their parent's kind, under ADR-CAT-039. They carry isActiveFl and the audit fields. Their code and names are either the parent's or are named in their own table.

Primary key naming follows `{entity}Pk`. Logical types are: text, number, decimal, flag, date-time, lookup, reference, identifier (BIGINT identity).

## A4 — Functional requirements (EARS) and acceptance criteria

### REQ-CAT-001 — One catalogue record for every channel
Pattern    : ubiquitous
Statement  : The system shall serve every Item and its Variants to the back-office, the point of sale and the storefront from one catalogue record per Item.
Traces     : US-CAT-001
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001, ENT-CAT-002
Rationale  : The owner never maintains the same Item in three places.
Source     : US-CAT-001; POL-CAT-001; KB:raw-idea.md §1
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-001 — [REQ-CAT-001]
Given : an Item with code ITM-000001 created from the back-office
When  : the point-of-sale read and the storefront read request that Item by its itemPk
Then  : both reads return the same itemPk, code ITM-000001 and nameAr as the back-office read, and the tenant holds exactly 1 Item record with that code

### REQ-CAT-002 — Read an Item
Pattern    : event
Statement  : When a caller requests an Item by its identifier, the system shall return the Item with its active Variants, Variant attribute assignments, Item units, barcodes, Bundle component lines and active media.
Traces     : US-CAT-001
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001, ENT-CAT-002, ENT-CAT-005, ENT-CAT-007, ENT-CAT-008, ENT-CAT-009, ENT-CAT-010
Rationale  : Every channel reads the whole catalogue entry in one request.
Source     : US-CAT-001
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-002 — [REQ-CAT-002]
Given : an Item with 2 active Variants, 1 inactive Variant, 1 active Item unit and 3 active barcodes
When  : a caller requests the Item by its itemPk
Then  : the response holds the Item, exactly the 2 active Variants, the 1 Item unit and the 3 barcodes

### REQ-CAT-003 — Search Items
Pattern    : event
Statement  : When a user searches Items with any of the filters text, Category, brand, kind, publish state and active state, the system shall return a page of the matching Items sorted by nameAr.
Traces     : US-CAT-001
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001
Rationale  : Back-office list of the single catalogue.
Source     : US-CAT-001
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-003 — [REQ-CAT-003]
Given : 30 Items, of which 12 belong to the Category «هواتف»
When  : a user searches with that Category as filter and a page size of 10
Then  : page 1 holds 10 Items, the total count is 12, and the Items are sorted by nameAr ascending

### REQ-CAT-004 — Update an Item
Pattern    : event
Statement  : When an owner submits changes to an active Item, the system shall save the changed nameAr, nameEn, categoryFk, brandCode, taxClassCode, descriptionAr and descriptionEn values.
Traces     : US-CAT-001
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001
Rationale  : The Item is changed once, in one place.
Source     : US-CAT-001
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-004 — [REQ-CAT-004]
Given : an active Item with an empty brandCode
When  : the owner sets brandCode to an active CAT_BRAND value and saves
Then  : the next read returns that brandCode, and updatedBy holds the owner's user identifier

### REQ-CAT-005 — Deactivate an Item
Pattern    : event
Statement  : When an owner deactivates an Item, the system shall set its isActiveFl to false and its publishStateCode to UNPUBLISHED.
Traces     : US-CAT-001
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001
Rationale  : An Item that is no longer sold leaves every channel but stays readable by earlier documents.
Source     : US-CAT-001; RULE-CAT-041; RULE-CAT-048
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-005 — [REQ-CAT-005]
Given : an active Item with publishStateCode PUBLISHED
When  : the owner deactivates it
Then  : isActiveFl is false, publishStateCode is UNPUBLISHED, a read by itemPk still returns the Item, and the response shows ar: «تم الإيقاف. يبقى السجل متاحًا للمستندات السابقة ولا يظهر في الاختيارات الجديدة.» · en: "Deactivated. The record stays available to earlier documents and no longer appears in new selections."

### REQ-CAT-006 — Reactivate an Item
Pattern    : event
Statement  : When an owner reactivates an inactive Item, the system shall set its isActiveFl to true and keep its publishStateCode UNPUBLISHED.
Traces     : US-CAT-001
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001
Rationale  : An Item comes back without reappearing on the storefront by accident.
Source     : US-CAT-001
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-006 — [REQ-CAT-006]
Given : an inactive Item
When  : the owner reactivates it
Then  : isActiveFl is true and publishStateCode is UNPUBLISHED

### REQ-CAT-007 — Same Item entered twice
Pattern    : unwanted
Statement  : If a create or update request gives an Item the nameAr of another active Item of the tenant, then the system shall reject the request with RULE-CAT-003.
Traces     : US-CAT-001
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001
Rationale  : Prevents one Item from being kept as two records.
Source     : US-CAT-001; D-13
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-007 — [REQ-CAT-007]
Given : an active Item named «شاحن سامسونج 25W»
When  : the owner creates another Item named «شاحن سامسونج 25W»
Then  : no Item is created and the response shows ar: «يوجد صنف نشط بالاسم نفسه. افتح الصنف الموجود أو غيّر الاسم.» · en: "An active item already has this name. Open the existing item or change the name." with the existing Item's code as the next action

### REQ-CAT-008 — Publish catalogue-change events
Pattern    : event
Statement  : When a change to an Item, Variant, Variant attribute assignment, Bundle component, Item unit, barcode or Item media is committed, the system shall publish one catalogue-change event carrying the record identifier, the itemPk and the change kind.
Traces     : US-CAT-002
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001, ENT-CAT-002, ENT-CAT-005, ENT-CAT-007, ENT-CAT-008, ENT-CAT-009, ENT-CAT-010
Rationale  : Downstream modules react to catalogue changes without CAT reading them.
Source     : US-CAT-002; module-registry AUTO "CAT publishes catalogue-change events"
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-008 — [REQ-CAT-008]
Given : an active Item
When  : the owner changes its nameEn and the change is committed
Then  : exactly 1 catalogue-change event is published, carrying the itemPk and change kind UPDATED

### REQ-CAT-009 — No event for a change that was not saved
Pattern    : unwanted
Statement  : If the transaction holding a catalogue change is rolled back, then the system shall publish no catalogue-change event for that change.
Traces     : US-CAT-002
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001, ENT-CAT-002
Rationale  : Channels never act on a change that does not exist.
Source     : US-CAT-002; RULE-CAT-049
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-009 — [REQ-CAT-009]
Given : an Item update whose commit fails
When  : the transaction is rolled back
Then  : 0 catalogue-change events are published and the response shows ar: «تعذّر حفظ التغيير ولم يُرسل أي إشعار بالتغيير. أعد المحاولة.» · en: "The change was not saved and no change notice was sent. Try again."

### REQ-CAT-010 — Committed change seen by every channel
Pattern    : ubiquitous
Statement  : The system shall return a committed catalogue change on every read that starts after the commit, whichever channel reads.
Traces     : US-CAT-002
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001, ENT-CAT-002
Rationale  : Every channel sells the same, current Item.
Source     : US-CAT-002
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-010 — [REQ-CAT-010]
Given : the owner changed an Item's nameEn from "Charger" to "Fast charger 25W" and the change is committed
When  : the point-of-sale read and the storefront read request the Item after the commit
Then  : both reads return nameEn "Fast charger 25W"

### REQ-CAT-011 — Behaviour follows capability flags only
Pattern    : ubiquitous
Statement  : The system shall decide every catalogue behaviour that differs between trading activities from the capability flags `variants` and `bundles`, read through the FeatureFlags API, and never from the tenant's activity or activity preset.
Traces     : US-CAT-003
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001, ENT-CAT-002
Rationale  : One codebase serves every activity (G1).
Source     : US-CAT-003; POL-CAT-002; KB:raw-idea.md §3 D2; ADR-CAT-041
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-011 — [REQ-CAT-011]
Given : two tenants with different activities and the same values of `variants` and `bundles`
When  : each tenant creates an Item with the same entries
Then  : both responses are equal in every field except itemPk, variantPk, code and the audit fields

### REQ-CAT-012 — Flag change applied at once
Pattern    : event
Statement  : When the value of the `variants` or `bundles` capability flag changes, the system shall apply the new value from the next catalogue request onward without a restart.
Traces     : US-CAT-003
Entities   : ENT-CAT-003, ENT-CAT-007
Rationale  : The owner adjusts flags at any time.
Source     : US-CAT-003; KB:raw-idea.md §6.2
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-012 — [REQ-CAT-012]
Given : the `variants` flag is off
When  : the owner turns it on and then creates a Variant attribute in the next request
Then  : the Variant attribute is created and returned with isActiveFl true

### REQ-CAT-013 — Flag value unreadable
Pattern    : unwanted
Statement  : If the value of a capability flag cannot be read, then the system shall refuse the operation that depends on that flag with RULE-CAT-009.
Traces     : US-CAT-003
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001, ENT-CAT-005, ENT-CAT-007
Rationale  : A flag-bound operation never runs on an unknown flag value.
Source     : US-CAT-003; ADR-CAT-041
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-013 — [REQ-CAT-013]
Given : the settings service does not answer the `bundles` flag read
When  : the owner saves an Item of kind BUNDLE
Then  : no record is saved and the response shows ar: «تعذّر التحقق من إعدادات القدرات الآن، ولم يُحفظ شيء. أعد المحاولة بعد لحظات.» · en: "Capability settings could not be checked, and nothing was saved. Try again in a moment."

### REQ-CAT-014 — Variant functions hidden while off
Pattern    : state
Statement  : While the `variants` capability flag is off, the system shall exclude the Variant attribute functions, the Variant attribute fields and the Variant list from every catalogue screen, menu and search filter.
Traces     : US-CAT-004
Entities   : ENT-CAT-002, ENT-CAT-003, ENT-CAT-004, ENT-CAT-005
Rationale  : No dead menus (G2).
Source     : US-CAT-004; POL-CAT-003; KB:raw-idea.md §4.1, §6.3
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-014 — [REQ-CAT-014]
Given : the `variants` flag is off
When  : a seller/cashier opens the Catalog menu and the Items screen
Then  : the menu holds no "Variant attributes" entry, and the Items screen shows 0 Variant attribute fields and 0 Variant list sections

### REQ-CAT-015 — Bundle functions hidden while off
Pattern    : state
Statement  : While the `bundles` capability flag is off, the system shall exclude the BUNDLE kind, the Bundle component lines and the bundle filter from every catalogue screen, menu and search filter.
Traces     : US-CAT-004
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001, ENT-CAT-007
Rationale  : No dead menus (G2).
Source     : US-CAT-004; POL-CAT-003
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-015 — [REQ-CAT-015]
Given : the `bundles` flag is off
When  : the owner opens the Item entry and the Item search
Then  : the kind field and the kind filter are absent, and 0 Bundle component sections are shown

### REQ-CAT-016 — Flag-off operation refused
Pattern    : unwanted
Statement  : If a request invokes a Variant attribute operation or a bundle operation while the capability flag that enables it is off, then the system shall reject the request with RULE-CAT-008.
Traces     : US-CAT-004
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001, ENT-CAT-003, ENT-CAT-005, ENT-CAT-007
Rationale  : Hidden functions also stay closed to direct calls.
Source     : US-CAT-004; POL-CAT-003
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-016 — [REQ-CAT-016]
Given : the `variants` flag is off
When  : a request creates a Variant attribute
Then  : nothing is created and the response shows ar: «هذه الوظيفة غير مفعّلة لنشاطك. فعّل مفتاح القدرة «variants» من الإعدادات لاستخدامها.» · en: "This function is not switched on for your business. Turn on the «variants» capability flag in settings to use it."

#### AC-CAT-017 — [REQ-CAT-016]
Given : the `bundles` flag is off
When  : a request creates an Item of kind BUNDLE
Then  : nothing is created and the response shows ar: «هذه الوظيفة غير مفعّلة لنشاطك. فعّل مفتاح القدرة «bundles» من الإعدادات لاستخدامها.» · en: "This function is not switched on for your business. Turn on the «bundles» capability flag in settings to use it."

### REQ-CAT-017 — Turning variants on keeps existing Items
Pattern    : event
Statement  : When the `variants` capability flag is turned on, the system shall keep every existing Item with its default Variant, codes, barcodes and identifiers unchanged.
Traces     : US-CAT-005
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001, ENT-CAT-002, ENT-CAT-009
Rationale  : No re-setup and no data move (G3, G8).
Source     : US-CAT-005; POL-CAT-004; KB:raw-idea.md §6.2, §6.8
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-017 — reserved
(See AC-CAT-018; this placeholder is not used.)

#### AC-CAT-018 — [REQ-CAT-017]
Given : 200 Items, each with only its default Variant, while `variants` is off
When  : the owner turns `variants` on
Then  : the 200 Items return the same itemPk, variantPk, codes and barcodes as before, and 0 of these records changes its updatedAt

### REQ-CAT-018 — Existing Variants take the first values
Pattern    : event
Statement  : When Variants are generated for an Item that already has Variants, the system shall assign the first selected value of each newly used attribute to the existing Variants before it creates Variants for the remaining combinations.
Traces     : US-CAT-005
Entities   : ENT-CAT-002, ENT-CAT-005
Rationale  : Items already in use grow variants without losing their identity.
Source     : US-CAT-005; RULE-CAT-012
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-019 — [REQ-CAT-018]
Given : an Item whose only Variant is its default Variant VAR-000010
When  : the owner selects the values S and M of the attribute «المقاس»
Then  : VAR-000010 carries the value S, 1 new Variant carries the value M, and the Item has 2 active Variants

### REQ-CAT-019 — Barcodes stay on the default Variant
Pattern    : unwanted
Statement  : If Variants are generated for an Item whose default Variant carries barcodes, then the system shall keep those barcodes on the default Variant and create the new Variants with no barcode.
Traces     : US-CAT-005
Entities   : ENT-CAT-002, ENT-CAT-009
Rationale  : Goods already labelled keep scanning to the same Variant.
Source     : US-CAT-005; RULE-CAT-012
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-020 — [REQ-CAT-019]
Given : a default Variant carrying the GTIN 6223000000014
When  : the owner generates 3 Variants for its Item
Then  : the GTIN 6223000000014 still belongs to the default Variant, the 2 new Variants carry 0 barcodes, and the response shows ar: «احتفظت المتغيّرات الحالية بأرقامها وباركوداتها وأُسندت إليها القيم الأولى المختارة.» · en: "Existing variants kept their codes and barcodes and took the first selected values."

### REQ-CAT-020 — Turning bundles on uses existing Variants
Pattern    : event
Statement  : When the `bundles` capability flag is turned on, the system shall accept every active Variant of an existing STANDARD Item as a Bundle component without changing that Variant.
Traces     : US-CAT-005
Entities   : ENT-CAT-002, ENT-CAT-007
Rationale  : Bundles are built from Items already sold.
Source     : US-CAT-005; POL-CAT-004
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-021 — [REQ-CAT-020]
Given : an active STANDARD Item created while `bundles` was off
When  : the owner turns `bundles` on and adds its Variant to a new Bundle
Then  : the Bundle is saved with 1 component line, and the component Variant's updatedAt is unchanged

### REQ-CAT-021 — Turn-off check
Pattern    : event
Statement  : When a turn-off check is requested for the `variants` or `bundles` capability flag, the system shall return whether the turn-off is allowed, the count of dependent Items and at most 20 dependent Items by code and nameAr.
Traces     : US-CAT-006
Entities   : ENT-CAT-001, ENT-CAT-002, ENT-CAT-005
Rationale  : The flag owner and the owner learn what blocks a turn-off and what to do next.
Source     : US-CAT-006; POL-CAT-005; ADR-CAT-041
Priority   : HIGH

#### AC-CAT-022 — [REQ-CAT-021]
Given : no active Item uses Variant attributes
When  : a turn-off check for `variants` is requested
Then  : the response returns allowed = true, a dependent count of 0 and an empty list

### REQ-CAT-022 — Variants turn-off blocked
Pattern    : unwanted
Statement  : If an active Variant attribute assignment exists on an active Variant of an active Item, then the system shall answer a turn-off check of the `variants` flag as not allowed with RULE-CAT-010.
